Conciliarists (and later Orthodox and Protestant readers): the Church in council stood above its failed head; ultramontane Catholic historians: an emergency tribunal filling a doubtful vacancy; *Haec Sancta*'s standing disputed | [5.4](lessons/05-04-avignon-the-great-schism-and-the-councils.md) |
| 1790–1815 | The Concordat: a pope removed an entire national episcopate by his own act, which no pope had done before | A gain in papal authority *within* the Church (Catholic Encyclopedia: "the most dazzling manifestation of the pope's supreme power"); begins the ultramontane turn | The Church's legal position in France was a surrender (state nominates, pays, keeps the lands; Organic Articles revive Gallican controls); the Civil Constitution had reduced the pope to a recipient of letters | [8.1](lessons/08-01-enlightenment-revolution-and-napoleon.md) |
| 1854–1870 | *Ineffabilis Deus* (1854) defined without a council; Vatican I (about 700 bishops and prelates from every continent) defines universal jurisdiction and infallibility (1870); within a few years every minority bishop accepted | The culmination of the thread: no earlier era produces so explicit a conciliar statement of Roman primacy. Catholic historians (Schatz, O'Malley): a development whose balance, the bishops, came only at Vatican II | The minority argued historically (Honorius, Constance, the churches' witness; many thought it inopportune); test vote 451–88–62; Hasler reads the council as unfree, Schatz defends its freedom; Orthodox theologians see the sharpest statement of the claim they reject, many Protestant historians centralization completed | [8.2](lessons/08-02-pius-ix-vatican-i-and-the-fall-of-rome.md) |

## Authority and citations

How the course cites its sources, and how to tell one kind of Church document from another. **This course never assigns a doctrinal level.** Where a claim's weight matters it carries the level stated by [`fundamental-theology`](../fundamental-theology/syllabus.md): the ladder is [4.4](../fundamental-theology/lessons/04-04-the-ladder-of-doctrinal-authority.md), reading a document's weight is [4.5](../fundamental-theology/lessons/04-05-reading-a-documents-weight.md), infallibility's conditions [4.2](../fundamental-theology/lessons/04-02-infallibility-and-its-conditions.md), religious submission [4.3](../fundamental-theology/lessons/04-03-religious-submission.md). Misstating a date or overstating a level is an exegetical error.

### Citation forms

| Source | Cited as | Example |
|---|---|---|
| Conciliar canons | Council, canon number | Chalcedon canon 28; Constantinople I canon 3; Lateran IV canons 3, 21, 67, 68; Clermont canon 2 |
| Trent's decrees | Session, decree, chapter (reform decrees have chapters; doctrinal decrees have canons, the numbered anathemas) | Session XXIII, reform decree, ch. 18 (the seminary decree, not "canon 18"); Session XXIV, ch. 1 (*Tametsi*) |
| Papal and curial documents | Incipit (opening Latin words), date, section | *Pascendi* §39; *Dignitatis Humanae* §§2, 7, 10, 12; *Dictatus Papae* 22; Syllabus proposition 79 |
| Roman dates | Counted inclusively backwards from Kalends, Nones or Ides | *quarto nonas Junii* = 2 June (Nones of June on the 5th), not MacNutt's 4 June |
| Fathers in translation | *Ante-Nicene Fathers* (ANF) or *Nicene and Post-Nicene Fathers* (NPNF, series/volume) | ANF 3 (Perpetua, Tertullian); ANF 7 (Lactantius); NPNF II/1 (*Life of Constantine*); II/4 (*Life of Antony*); II/10 (Ambrose); II/12 (Leo's letters); II/14 (canons and acts of the councils) |
| Eusebius, *Church History* (*HE*) | Book.chapter.section | *HE* 5.23–24 (Victor); 3.5.3 (Pella); 10.5 (Licinius's letter) |
| Bede, *Ecclesiastical History* | Book.chapter, roman book numeral | I.30 (letter to Mellitus); I.32; II.5; III.25 (Whitby) |
| Gregory I, *Register* | Book.letter (numbering differs by edition) | VIII.29 (VIII.30 in the Schaff translation); I.47; IV.26; IX.65 |
| Classical authors | Work book.chapter | Tacitus, *Annals* 15.44; Suetonius, *Nero* 16.2; Pliny, *Letters* 10.96–97; Lactantius, *Deaths* 34, 44, 48 |
| Roman law | Code, book.title.law | Theodosian Code 16.1.2 (*Cunctos populos*) |
| Aquinas | *ST* part, question | *ST* II-II q.40 (war) |

### Kinds of document

- **Conciliar definition** vs **disciplinary canon** vs **acclamation**: Chalcedon's definition is defined dogma; canon 28 and Clermont's canon 2 are discipline; "Peter has spoken through Leo" is a shout on the minutes with no weight of its own. *([2.2](lessons/02-02-ephesus-chalcedon-and-leos-rome.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-the-crusades.md))*
- **Bull** vs **brief**: *Zelo domus Dei* (1648) and *Dominus ac Redemptor* (1773) are briefs; *Pastorale officium* (1537) was a brief that could be annulled without annulling the bull *Sublimis Deus* (whether the bull fell with it is disputed). *([6.2](lessons/06-02-reformations-beyond-wittenberg.md), [7.1](lessons/07-01-conquest-and-conscience-in-the-americas.md), [7.2](lessons/07-02-accommodation-in-asia.md))*
- **Decretal**: a papal letter with force of law (*Per venerabilem*, 1202; *Ad abolendam*, 1184). **Register entry**: the *Dictatus Papae* was never sent or published, so it is evidence of the curia's mind, not law. *([4.1](lessons/04-01-the-reform-papacy-and-the-investiture-contest.md), [5.1](lessons/05-01-papal-monarchy-innocent-iii-to-boniface-viii.md))*
- **Congregation decrees and sentences**: the Index decree of 1616 (disciplinary, approved by Paul V), the Holy Office consultors' opinion (unpublished advice), the 1633 sentence (judicial), the 1822 permission; the Holy Office decree on communism (1949, a penalty); *Lamentabili* (1907, Holy Office decree); *Plane compertum est* (1939, an instruction of Propaganda Fide). None is a council or a papal definition. *([7.3](lessons/07-03-the-galileo-affair.md), [8.3](lessons/08-03-leo-xiii-and-the-modernist-crisis.md), [9.1](lessons/09-01-the-church-and-the-totalitarian-states.md), [7.2](lessons/07-02-accommodation-in-asia.md))*
- **Encyclical**: most encyclical teaching sits at the authentic, non-definitive rung and is owed religious submission; *Humanae Vitae*'s level is disputed (Ford and Grisez argue infallible through the ordinary universal magisterium). **Motu proprio**: a papal act on the pope's own initiative (*Sacrorum antistitum*, 1910; *Vos estis lux mundi*, 2019; *Tra le sollecitudini*, 1903). *([9.1](lessons/09-01-the-church-and-the-totalitarian-states.md), [9.3](lessons/09-03-after-the-council.md), [8.3](lessons/08-03-leo-xiii-and-the-modernist-crisis.md))*
- **Solemn papal definition**: *Ineffabilis Deus* (1854), rung 1, defined without a council. **Imposed formula**: the anti-modernist oath (required until 1967) is not a definition. **Reform decree**: *Tametsi* binds but sits on no doctrinal rung, and was replaced by *Ne temere* (1907). *([8.2](lessons/08-02-pius-ix-vatican-i-and-the-fall-of-rome.md), [8.3](lessons/08-03-leo-xiii-and-the-modernist-crisis.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-trent-and-catholic-reform.md))*
- **Phrases that do not settle weight**: "we define and declare" in *Sublimis Deus*; "from the fullness of apostolic power" in *Regnans in Excelsis*; "anathema" in a bull's sanction clause. The deposing power was a claim of the medieval canonists, never defined by a council. *([7.1](lessons/07-01-conquest-and-conscience-in-the-americas.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-the-english-reformations.md))*
- **Contested standing, left open here**: *Haec Sancta* (standing rule, emergency measure, or invalid); *Unam Sanctam*'s closing sentence; whether *Exiit* and *Cum inter nonnullos* contradict; *Charitas*; *Ex illa die* and the 1939 reversal (disciplinary judgements of fact). *([5.1](lessons/05-01-papal-monarchy-innocent-iii-to-boniface-viii.md), [5.2](lessons/05-02-friars-and-universities.md), [5.4](lessons/05-04-avignon-the-great-schism-and-the-councils.md), [8.1](lessons/08-01-enlightenment-revolution-and-napoleon.md))*

## Timeline

Dates as the lessons give them, with their hedges, in date order. The ten hinge dates the course is built around are in bold: **312** (Milvian Bridge: an emperor's patronage), **451** (Chalcedon: a council's contest with an imperial see), **800** (a pope crowns an emperor), **1054** (the breach with the East, as symbol), **1122** (Worms: a settlement with the emperor), **1517** (the revolt against Rome's authority), **1545** (Trent: a council's answer), **1789** (the Revolution that nearly destroyed the Church), **1870** (the loss of the temporal power), **1965** (a council that met the modern world without a throne behind it). The closing section of [9.3](lessons/09-03-after-the-council.md) reads them as changes in the Church's relation to power.

## Numbers

Contested figures exactly as the lessons state them, with their hedges. Do not harden a range into a point.

### Martyr counts

- **Great Persecution:** Eusebius's *Martyrs of Palestine* names 83 by one modern count, 92 by Gibbon's (depending on recension and what counts). Gibbon (*Decline and Fall*, ch. 16) treated Palestine as a sixteenth of the East, extrapolated about 1,500 there, and reached "somewhat less than two thousand" overall. Frend (*The Rise of Christianity*, 1984): roughly 3,000–3,500. Eusebius saw days in the Thebaid with dozens and once about a hundred killed (*Church History* 8.9), so Egypt was probably hit harder. The lesson's honest statement: **thousands, probably low thousands, for the Great Persecution; the whole period's total is unknown.** No assumption gets it near six figures. For Decius and Valerian no one can do more than list known names.
- **Decian certificates:** about 46 surviving *libelli*, nearly all June–July 250.

*From* [1.3](lessons/01-03-persecution-and-martyrdom.md)

### Stark growth model

Rodney Stark's estimate of how fast Christians multiplied: 40 percent per decade from about 1,000 in the year 40.

$$N(t) = 1000 \times 1.4^{(t-40)/10}$$

where $N(t)$ is the number of Christians in year $t$. The annual factor is $1.4^{1/10} \approx 1.0342$, i.e. **3.42 percent a year** (Stark's own figure); doubling time $\ln 2 / (\ln 1.4/10) \approx 20.6$ years. Dividing 40 by 10 to get 4 percent ignores compounding. (*The Rise of Christianity*, 1996. Checking the model against the year 300 and against the chronology of persecution is Boss 1.)

*Introduced:* [1.4](lessons/01-04-constantine.md)

### Early church and early medieval figures

- Marcion's returned gift: 200,000 sesterces (Tertullian). *([1.2](lessons/01-02-bishops-canon-and-the-parting-of-the-ways.md))*
- **Nicaea's attendance:** more than 250 (Eusebius of Caesarea), about 300 and later 318 (Athanasius); only two bishops refused to sign. **Constantinople 381:** about 150 eastern bishops. *([2.1](lessons/02-01-nicaea-and-the-imperial-church.md))*
- **Thessalonica, 390:** number unknown; Theodoret, about sixty years later, reports 7,000 "were said to have died". *([2.1](lessons/02-01-nicaea-and-the-imperial-church.md))*
- **Athanasius in exile:** about 197 months (16.4 years) of a 539-month episcopate, roughly 37 percent; older books say seventeen years. *([2.1](lessons/02-01-nicaea-and-the-imperial-church.md))*
- **Pachomian houses at his death:** nine for men and two for women (Catholic Encyclopedia); some modern accounts fewer. *([2.3](lessons/02-03-the-desert-and-the-monastery.md))*
- **Kent, Christmas 597:** "more than ten thousand" English *reported* baptized (Gregory, second-hand). *([3.1](lessons/03-01-gregory-the-great-and-the-conversion-of-the-peoples.md))*
- **Verden, 782:** 4,500 in the Royal Frankish Annals; disputed (Becher: far fewer executions plus deportations; others accept the figure). *([3.1](lessons/03-01-gregory-the-great-and-the-conversion-of-the-peoples.md))*
- **Cordoba, 850–859:** forty-eight executed. *([3.2](lessons/03-02-the-rise-of-islam-and-the-christian-mediterranean.md))*
- **Muslim majorities:** Iran ninth century; Egypt, Syria, Iraq around the tenth (Bulliet); Egypt perhaps as late as the fourteenth. *([3.2](lessons/03-02-the-rise-of-islam-and-the-christian-mediterranean.md))*
- **Rhineland 1096:** Speyer about a dozen; Worms about 800 (Hebrew chronicles); Mainz about 1,100 (Hebrew chronicle) or about 700 (Albert of Aachen); total estimates start at about two thousand, not closely checkable. *([4.3](lessons/04-03-the-crusades.md))*
- **Jerusalem 1099:** Fulcher almost ten thousand in the Temple; Ibn al-Athir seventy thousand (a century later; probably more than the whole population); Ibn al-Arabi about three thousand in al-Aqsa. *([4.3](lessons/04-03-the-crusades.md))*
- **Constantinople 1182:** Latins massacred, hundreds to thousands (disputed). *([4.2](lessons/04-02-east-and-west-the-long-estrangement.md))*
### Inquisition numbers

Medieval figures here; the early modern tribunals' figures are added below from 6.5.

- **Bernard Gui, Toulouse, 1308–1323** (*Book of Sentences*): about 636 people convicted on some 900 or more counts; at most 45 handed to the secular arm and burned (some counts 41 or 42, depending on how the relapsed and dead are classed), i.e. about 6.4 to 7.1 percent; some 307 imprisoned (48.3 percent) and 143 sentenced to wear crosses. A low execution rate is not a low punishment rate. *([5.3](lessons/05-03-heresy-inquisition-and-the-jews.md))*
- **Fournier, Pamiers, 1318–1325:** 578 interrogations, five burned. *([5.3](lessons/05-03-heresy-inquisition-and-the-jews.md))*
- **Toulouse 1245–1246:** more than five thousand questioned from the Lauragais. *([5.3](lessons/05-03-heresy-inquisition-and-the-jews.md))*
### Inquisition death toll estimates

Executions in person by the Spanish Inquisition, by estimator. Every serious estimate is in the thousands; none in the hundreds of thousands; "millions" has never had documentary basis.

| Who | When | Executions in person (Spain) | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|
| Llorente (secretary at Madrid 1789–91) | 1817–18 | 31,912 (plus 17,659 in effigy, 291,450 penanced), 1481–1808 | Assumed annual average per tribunal, multiplied out |
| Lea | 1906–07 | No total; showed Llorente far above surviving records (Toledo 1483–1501: records 297, Llorente 666; Saragossa 1485–1502: 124 vs 584; Valladolid 1485–92: 50 vs 424) | Tribunal archives |
| Henningsen and Contreras | 1986 | 826 in person, 778 in effigy, in 44,674 recorded cases, 1540–1700 (about 1.8 percent); Henningsen later estimated about 87,000 trials in the period | Annual reports to the Suprema (incomplete) |
| Kamen; Monter | 1990s | About 2,000 before 1530; around 3,000 in all (Monter 2,750) | Local studies plus the report series |
| Perez; Dedieu | 1987–2005 | Fewer than 10,000, most before about 1530 | Estimated total trials (125,000–200,000) and early rates |
| Del Col | 2010 | About 12,000 (about 10,500 before 1530) | Assumes 15 percent of early trials ended in execution |

After 1540 the estimates agree (same reports); before 1530 they diverge by a factor of five (records largely lost). The 1.8 percent rate is post-1540 and cannot be applied back to 1480–1530; its denominator is mostly blasphemy, bigamy and loose talk. **Roman Inquisition:** about 1,250 death sentences in some 51,000–75,000 cases (Del Col). **Witch trials in Europe:** an estimated 40,000–60,000 killed (Levack), overwhelmingly by local and secular courts. Lea, the fiercest archival historian, rejected the inflated totals in 1907 (vol. 4, p. 517).

*From* [6.5](lessons/06-05-the-spanish-and-roman-inquisitions.md)

### High medieval figures

- **Beziers 1209:** "nearly 20,000" dead (Arnaud's own report); town population about 10,000–14,500 (modern estimates), some escaped; toll in the thousands. Caesarius: "more than a hundred thousand men" (inflated). **Montsegur 1244:** about 200 burned. *([5.3](lessons/05-03-heresy-inquisition-and-the-jews.md))*
- **Jews of England 1290:** roughly 2,000–3,000. **Paris 1242:** some twenty-four cartloads of manuscripts. *([5.3](lessons/05-03-heresy-inquisition-and-the-jews.md))*
- **Lateran IV:** some 400 bishops and over 800 abbots and priors. **John's tribute:** 1,000 marks a year. *([5.1](lessons/05-01-papal-monarchy-innocent-iii-to-boniface-viii.md))*
- **Marseille 1318:** four Spiritual Franciscans burned. *([5.2](lessons/05-02-friars-and-universities.md))*
### Reformation figures

- **Peasants' War:** around 100,000 dead (commonly cited, rough). **St Bartholomew 1572:** perhaps 2,000–3,000 in Paris, thousands more in the provinces (disputed). **Thirty Years' War:** Empire lost roughly a fifth of its population (Wilson; others higher). *([6.2](lessons/06-02-reformations-beyond-wittenberg.md))*
- **England:** over 800 religious houses closed; Pilgrimage of Grace perhaps 30,000 men, about 216 executed; Marian burnings about 280–290 (usual counts 283–287), February 1555–November 1558; Elizabethan executions about 189 Catholics (some 126 priests, 63 lay), plus several hundred after the Northern Rising (the queen demanded at least 700). Rates depend on the years counted: Mary's per-year rate runs roughly 7 to 17 times Elizabeth's depending on the pairing. *([6.3](lessons/06-03-the-english-reformations.md))*
- **Trent:** about thirty prelates at the opening (1545); more than two hundred signed (1563). **France:** about sixteen seminaries 1580–1620 for more than a hundred dioceses. *([6.4](lessons/06-04-trent-and-catholic-reform.md))*
- **Sermon on Indulgence and Grace:** about fourteen reprints in 1518. *([6.1](lessons/06-01-the-late-medieval-church-and-luthers-protest.md))*
### Early modern figures

- **Americas:** 1492 hemisphere about 8 million (Kroeber) to over 100 million (Dobyns); Denevan roughly 43–65 million; central Mexico about 25 million (1519) to near one million (1605) (Cook and Borah; start contested); fall of something like nine-tenths within a century on most estimates. Las Casas's African slave licence: 4,000. *([7.1](lessons/07-01-conquest-and-conscience-in-the-americas.md))*
- **Asia:** perhaps 300,000 Christians in Japan by about 1600 (estimate); 26 crucified at Nagasaki, 1597; about 200,000 Christians in China by 1700 (missionaries' counts). *([7.2](lessons/07-02-accommodation-in-asia.md))*
- **Galileo:** eleven consultors (1616); seven of ten cardinal judges signed (1633); Copernicus on the Index 219 years (1616–1835). *([7.3](lessons/07-03-the-galileo-affair.md))*
### Modern figures

- **French Church 1789:** tithes plus somewhere between about 6 and 10 percent of the land; about 130 dioceses reduced to 83. **Oath:** seven bishops; parish jurors roughly half (up to 55–60 percent). **September massacres:** about 1,100–1,600 killed, over two hundred priests (191 beatified). **Vendee:** about 170,000 dead on the standard estimate (others vary widely). **Old-regime bishops refusing to resign in 1801:** about 36 to 45. *([8.1](lessons/08-01-enlightenment-revolution-and-napoleon.md))*
- **Vatican I:** about 700 at the first public session; 13 July 1870: 601 voting, 451–88–62 (75.0, 14.6, 10.3 percent); 55 to 60 minority left; 18 July: 535 present, 533–2. **Law of Guarantees:** about 3.2 million lire a year. *([8.2](lessons/08-02-pius-ix-vatican-i-and-the-fall-of-rome.md))*
- **Modernism:** *Lamentabili* 65 propositions; Sodalitium never more than about fifty. *([8.3](lessons/08-03-leo-xiii-and-the-modernist-crisis.md))*
### Twentieth-century figures

- **Lateran Financial Convention:** 750 million lire cash plus 1 billion lire in 5 percent bonds (50 million a year, about 15.5 times the 1871 offer in nominal lire; prices had risen steeply). **Vatican City:** about 44 hectares. *([9.1](lessons/09-01-the-church-and-the-totalitarian-states.md))*
- **Spain:** 6,832 clergy and religious killed in the Republican zone (Montero Moreno); Basque priests shot by Nationalists about fourteen to sixteen. **Netherlands 1942:** about 200–250 Catholics of Jewish descent arrested. **Rome 1943:** 1,259 arrested, 1,023 deported, sixteen returned; some 155 religious houses sheltered about 4,300, more than 3,200 of them Jews. ***Mit brennender Sorge*:** some 300,000 copies, twelve presses seized. *([9.1](lessons/09-01-the-church-and-the-totalitarian-states.md))*
- **Vatican II:** about 2,400 bishops at the opening; revelation motion 1,368–822 (19 invalid) of 2,209, short of two-thirds (1,473 of all ballots or 1,460 of valid; some print 1,360); final votes *Sacrosanctum Concilium* 2,147–4, *Lumen Gentium* 2,151–5, *Nostra Aetate* 2,221–88, *Dignitatis Humanae* 2,308–70; majority petition of 441 signatures (1964); Wiltgen: 79 of 109 slate candidates elected. *([9.2](lessons/09-02-the-second-vatican-council.md))*
- **Abuse:** see [John Jay reports](#john-jay-reports). **Solidarity:** some ten million members within a year. *([9.3](lessons/09-03-after-the-council.md))*

## Pitfalls

### Sources and dating

- **"Later" is not "false", "early" is not "true."** Paul is early but a party to every quarrel he reports; Josephus is late but has no stake in James. Weigh proximity and interest separately. *([1.1](lessons/01-01-the-first-generation-and-its-sources.md))*
- **Tacitus was not an eyewitness** of 64: born about 56, writing c. 115–120. *([1.1](lessons/01-01-the-first-generation-and-its-sources.md))*
- **"In hoc signo vinces" is not Eusebius's wording**; he wrote Greek, "conquer by this". *([1.4](lessons/01-04-constantine.md))*
- **Reading Acts as a source is not a doctrinal problem**: the historian asks only what each text can establish. *([1.1](lessons/01-01-the-first-generation-and-its-sources.md))*
- **Acclamations are not votes**: "Peter has spoken through Leo" is a shout on the minutes, not a decree. *([2.2](lessons/02-02-ephesus-chalcedon-and-leos-rome.md))*
- **Don't trust the church-door scene**: Ambrose's letter is private; the confrontation comes from Sozomen and Theodoret half a century later. The penance itself is well attested. *([2.1](lessons/02-01-nicaea-and-the-imperial-church.md))*
- **An author's silence about a source is not proof he didn't use it** (Benedict and the Rule of the Master). *([2.3](lessons/02-03-the-desert-and-the-monastery.md))*
- **Women were not an afterthought**; the problem is that we see Macrina and Paula almost only through men who wrote about them. *([2.3](lessons/02-03-the-desert-and-the-monastery.md))*
- **Hagiography is a source with a purpose**: the Geismar oak comes from a *Life* written about forty-five years later. *([3.1](lessons/03-01-gregory-the-great-and-the-conversion-of-the-peoples.md))*
- **A forgery is good evidence of its own time**, not of the time it claims. *([3.3](lessons/03-03-popes-franks-and-emperors.md))*
- **A hostile source is not a false source**: discount Liutprand's testimony, not the facts others confirm. **Motive does not touch inspectable evidence** (Valla). *([3.3](lessons/03-03-popes-franks-and-emperors.md))*
- **Don't read the Pact of Umar as a document of 638.** *([3.2](lessons/03-02-the-rise-of-islam-and-the-christian-mediterranean.md))*
- **"Welcomed the Arabs" and "persecuted by the Arabs" are both too simple**; the welcome evidence is late and partisan (Baladhuri, Michael the Syrian). *([3.2](lessons/03-02-the-rise-of-islam-and-the-christian-mediterranean.md))*
- **Canon versus chronicler**: the canon is the council's legislation; Fulcher's post-1099 version is evidence of what crusaders believed they were offered. *([4.3](lessons/04-03-the-crusades.md))*
- **"Blood to the bridles" is a biblical trope** (Rev. 14:20), not a depth measurement; deflating the numbers does not make the massacre a legend. *([4.3](lessons/04-03-the-crusades.md))*
- **A one-sided martyrology** (1204 without 1182) is how the estrangement reproduced itself. *([4.2](lessons/04-02-east-and-west-the-long-estrangement.md))*
- **Attributed is not attested**: the Tetzel couplet, "Here I stand", "Paris is well worth a Mass". *([6.1](lessons/06-01-the-late-medieval-church-and-luthers-protest.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-reformations-beyond-wittenberg.md))*
- **Two Johanns**: Eck of Leipzig (1519) is not von der Ecken of Worms (1521). *([6.1](lessons/06-01-the-late-medieval-church-and-luthers-protest.md))*
- **Mind the document's genre**: *Zelo domus Dei* is a brief, not a bull; the seminary decree is a chapter, not a canon. *([6.2](lessons/06-02-reformations-beyond-wittenberg.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-trent-and-catholic-reform.md))*
- **Law is not practice**: Burgos, *Sublimis Deus* and the New Laws are strong evidence of what was argued, weak evidence of what happened. *([7.1](lessons/07-01-conquest-and-conscience-in-the-americas.md))*
- **Authentic is not accurate or legitimate** (the 1616 minute); **"Eppur si muove"** first appears in 1757. *([7.3](lessons/07-03-the-galileo-affair.md))*
- **Check the translation**: MacNutt's "4 June" for *quarto nonas Junii* is 2 June; Kangxi reacted to a court translation of the bull. *([7.1](lessons/07-01-conquest-and-conscience-in-the-americas.md), [7.2](lessons/07-02-accommodation-in-asia.md))*
- **A witness formed from childhood by one side** (Edgardo Mortara) is good evidence of what he came to believe, poor evidence of whether the act was just; check an edition against its manuscript. *([8.2](lessons/08-02-pius-ix-vatican-i-and-the-fall-of-rome.md))*
- **A document may define a position so as to condemn it**; whether anyone held it is a separate question (Americanism, modernism). *([8.3](lessons/08-03-leo-xiii-and-the-modernist-crisis.md))*
- **A partisan source can still be used** for what its author could document (Wiltgen's tallies); treat its framing as one reading. *([9.2](lessons/09-02-the-second-vatican-council.md))*
- **Civil reports have scope**: the Murphy Report studied one archdiocese's handling from its own files, a sample of 46 of 102 priests; it cannot give national rates or causes. *([9.3](lessons/09-03-after-the-council.md))*

### Anachronism

- **Acts 15 is not "the first ecumenical council"**: one community's leaders with delegates from Antioch, and the two accounts disagree on what it decided. *([1.1](lessons/01-01-the-first-generation-and-its-sources.md))*
- **"Pope Clement" is a later office projected backwards**; Irenaeus's list is a document of 180. *([1.2](lessons/01-02-bishops-canon-and-the-parting-of-the-ways.md))*
- **One word, two offices:** before about 150, "bishop" may mean presbyter (1 Clement 42–44). *([1.2](lessons/01-02-bishops-canon-and-the-parting-of-the-ways.md))*
- **"Benedict founded Western monasticism"**: Martin, Lerins, Cassian and Bethlehem came earlier; the Rule became the norm only in 816–817. "*Ora et labora*" is not in the Rule. *([2.3](lessons/02-03-the-desert-and-the-monastery.md))*
- **"Semi-Pelagian" is a later label**; calling Cassian a Pelagian in any form is a verdict. *([2.3](lessons/02-03-the-desert-and-the-monastery.md))*
- **"Monophysite" is not a neutral synonym for non-Chalcedonian**; say miaphysite. *([2.2](lessons/02-02-ephesus-chalcedon-and-leos-rome.md))*
- **The letter to Mellitus is not religious tolerance** or modern inculturation: the idols are still destroyed. *([3.1](lessons/03-01-gregory-the-great-and-the-conversion-of-the-peoples.md))*
- **Top-down is not the same as forced**: Saxony is the exception the popular picture turns into the rule. *([3.1](lessons/03-01-gregory-the-great-and-the-conversion-of-the-peoples.md))*
- **Beware survivals**: a folk practice under a Christian name is not automatically pagan. *([3.1](lessons/03-01-gregory-the-great-and-the-conversion-of-the-peoples.md))*
- **"Conquered" is not "converted"**: conquest 630s–710s, Muslim majorities centuries later. *([3.2](lessons/03-02-the-rise-of-islam-and-the-christian-mediterranean.md))*
- **The *Dictatus Papae* is not a decree**, and proposition 22 is not a definition of papal infallibility. *([4.1](lessons/04-01-the-reform-papacy-and-the-investiture-contest.md))*
- **"Investiture" was the flashpoint, not the whole quarrel**; the first certain general ban is 1078, after the break. *([4.1](lessons/04-01-the-reform-papacy-and-the-investiture-contest.md))*
- **Friars are not monks.** *([5.2](lessons/05-02-friars-and-universities.md))*
- **"The pope founded the University of Paris"**: the guild existed before 1231. *([5.2](lessons/05-02-friars-and-universities.md))*
- **"1277 condemned Aquinas"**: he was not named; a local episcopal act, partly revoked 1325. *([5.2](lessons/05-02-friars-and-universities.md))*
- **"John XXII condemned poverty"**: he condemned a claim about Christ's legal condition asserted pertinaciously; Franciscans still vowed poverty. *([5.2](lessons/05-02-friars-and-universities.md))*
- **Decay at the top is not decay throughout.** **1517 was not a break**: Luther appealed to the pope against the preachers; the break came 1519–1521. *([6.1](lessons/06-01-the-late-medieval-church-and-luthers-protest.md))*
- **Augsburg was not toleration**, and *cuius regio* is not in it. **Munster is not "the Anabaptists".** *([6.2](lessons/06-02-reformations-beyond-wittenberg.md))*
- **"Henry VIII made England Protestant"**: he repudiated the pope, not the Mass. **Don't read the bull back into 1569.** *([6.3](lessons/06-03-the-english-reformations.md))*
- **Trent did not write the Missal, Catechism or Index**; **reform decrees are discipline, not dogma**; **the Jesuits were not founded to fight Luther.** *([6.4](lessons/06-04-trent-and-catholic-reform.md))*
- **"The Church" is not one actor** in the Indies: Rome, a crown-nominated bishop, a Dominican convent and a cleric-encomendero took opposite sides. *([7.1](lessons/07-01-conquest-and-conscience-in-the-americas.md))*
- **"Inculturation" is anachronism** for Jesuit accommodation, which worked top-down and tolerated caste separation. *([7.2](lessons/07-02-accommodation-in-asia.md))*
- **Jurors were not apostates**; **overreading Tackett** turns a regional correlation into a claim about motives. *([8.1](lessons/08-01-enlightenment-revolution-and-napoleon.md))*
- **Old Catholics are not Dollinger's church.** *([8.2](lessons/08-02-pius-ix-vatican-i-and-the-fall-of-rome.md))*
- **Leo XIII was not a liberal reversed by Pius X**; ***Pascendi* did not attack natural science**; **don't date the informers to 1907.** *([8.3](lessons/08-03-leo-xiii-and-the-modernist-crisis.md))*

### Early persecution and toleration

- **Nero's persecution was local** to Rome; no empire-wide law in 64. *([1.1](lessons/01-01-the-first-generation-and-its-sources.md))*
- **"Persecuted" is not "always persecuted."** Legal exposure was constant from Trajan on; state-initiated persecution covers roughly 250–251, 257–260 and 303–313. *([1.3](lessons/01-03-persecution-and-martyrdom.md))*
- **The catacombs were cemeteries, not hideouts**; Valerian banned access to them because they were known places. *([1.3](lessons/01-03-persecution-and-martyrdom.md))*
- **Martyr numbers cut both ways**: "thousands" corrects "hundreds of thousands" without erasing the executions, mines and confiscations. *([1.3](lessons/01-03-persecution-and-martyrdom.md))*
- **Galerius's edict (311) is not the "Edict of Milan" (313)**, and neither made Christianity the state religion; Constantine did not "legalize" it first. *([1.3](lessons/01-03-persecution-and-martyrdom.md), [1.4](lessons/01-04-constantine.md))*
- **The deathbed baptism is weak evidence either way**; the Silvester baptism legend is not history. *([1.4](lessons/01-04-constantine.md))*

### Primacy, authority and doctrinal weight

- **"Attempted" is not "accomplished"**: Eusebius says Victor *attempted* to cut Asia off; no source says it took effect. *([1.2](lessons/01-02-bishops-canon-and-the-parting-of-the-ways.md))*
- **The parting did not happen in one year**: not 70, not 135. *([1.2](lessons/01-02-bishops-canon-and-the-parting-of-the-ways.md))*
- **Convening is not defining**: emperors called councils and exiled bishops but could not make a creed stick; "caesaropapism" fits 325–381 badly. *([2.1](lessons/02-01-nicaea-and-the-imperial-church.md))*
- **Nicaea did not settle the controversy**; the settlement was 380–381, with a new emperor. *([2.1](lessons/02-01-nicaea-and-the-imperial-church.md))*
- **Don't say "Chalcedon split the Church in 451"**: separate hierarchies formed over a century (*Henotikon*, Acacian schism, Justinian). *([2.2](lessons/02-02-ephesus-chalcedon-and-leos-rome.md))*
- **Don't read Gelasius as claiming to rule the emperor's affairs**: he concedes the emperor's supremacy in public order. *([2.2](lessons/02-02-ephesus-chalcedon-and-leos-rome.md))*
- **The forged Donation did not create the Papal States**; Pepin's grant of 756 did. *([3.3](lessons/03-03-popes-franks-and-emperors.md))*
- **"The pope made Pepin king" overstates it**: the Franks elected him; the papacy sanctioned and anointed. *([3.3](lessons/03-03-popes-franks-and-emperors.md))*
- **Pseudo-Isidore was not a papal forgery.** *([3.3](lessons/03-03-popes-franks-and-emperors.md))*
- **Worms did not end royal influence over bishops**; it changed the symbols and the order of steps. *([4.1](lessons/04-01-the-reform-papacy-and-the-investiture-contest.md))*
- **"In 1054 the pope excommunicated the Orthodox Church"**: Leo IX was dead, the target was one patriarch, the counter-sentence hit three legates. **"1965 ended the schism"**: it lifted sentences, not the division. *([4.2](lessons/04-02-east-and-west-the-long-estrangement.md))*
- **Signed is not received**: Greek signatures under "full power" at Florence show what a delegation accepted under military pressure. *([4.2](lessons/04-02-east-and-west-the-long-estrangement.md))*
- **"Innocent ruled Europe"**: he could judge and pressure, not command armies; weigh each intervention by who else wanted the same result. *([5.1](lessons/05-01-papal-monarchy-innocent-iii-to-boniface-viii.md))*
- **Boniface did not simply lose to force**: he retreated on taxation in 1297, before any violence. *([5.1](lessons/05-01-papal-monarchy-innocent-iii-to-boniface-viii.md))*
- **Don't grade *Unam Sanctam*'s or *Haec Sancta*'s authority here.** *([5.1](lessons/05-01-papal-monarchy-innocent-iii-to-boniface-viii.md), [5.4](lessons/05-04-avignon-the-great-schism-and-the-councils.md))*
- **Avignon was not France**, and the popes were not simply the king's men; the Chinon parchment is not an acquittal of the order. *([5.4](lessons/05-04-avignon-the-great-schism-and-the-councils.md))*
- **Conciliarism was not anti-papal**: Constance wanted a pope, elected one, and burned Hus. *([5.4](lessons/05-04-avignon-the-great-schism-and-the-councils.md))*
- **Don't assign a doctrinal weight** to *Sublimis Deus* ("we define and declare"), *Ex illa die*, the 1616 decree or the 1633 sentence; "the Church defined geocentrism" is false. *([7.1](lessons/07-01-conquest-and-conscience-in-the-americas.md), [7.2](lessons/07-02-accommodation-in-asia.md), [7.3](lessons/07-03-the-galileo-affair.md))*
- **Rome did not ban accommodation**; **the suppression was not a verdict on the rites**; **1939 did not say 1715 was wrong.** *([7.2](lessons/07-02-accommodation-in-asia.md))*
- **"Vatican I made every papal word infallible"** is an exegetical error; **"533 to 2 means near-unanimity"** ignores the test vote; **the council was suspended, not ended.** *([8.2](lessons/08-02-pius-ix-vatican-i-and-the-fall-of-rome.md))*
- **Don't grade *Charitas* or the anti-modernist oath** as doctrinal definitions. *([8.1](lessons/08-01-enlightenment-revolution-and-napoleon.md), [8.3](lessons/08-03-leo-xiii-and-the-modernist-crisis.md))*
- **An encyclical is not a definition**; the 1949 decree imposes a penalty, not a dogmatic condemnation. **Vatican II defined no dogma**, but its dogmatic constitutions restate teaching at high weight; neither "changed doctrine" nor "only pastoral". **Don't overstate *Humanae Vitae*'s level either way.** *([9.1](lessons/09-01-the-church-and-the-totalitarian-states.md), [9.2](lessons/09-02-the-second-vatican-council.md), [9.3](lessons/09-03-after-the-council.md))*
- **The revelation schema was not voted down**: the motion to interrupt failed; the pope withdrew it. **Paul VI was nobody's rubber stamp.** **The 1965 declaration did not end the schism.** *([9.2](lessons/09-02-the-second-vatican-council.md))*

### Violence, persecution and acknowledgement

- **The indulgence was not "salvation for killing"**, and the opposite overcorrection is as wrong: preachers promised more and crusaders believed it. *([4.3](lessons/04-03-the-crusades.md))*
- **The Rhineland massacres were neither papal policy nor "just a mob"**: bishops tried to shelter Jews, but the attackers were crusaders and protection often failed. *([4.3](lessons/04-03-the-crusades.md))*
- **Don't let the acknowledgement stand in for the facts**: the Day of Pardon does not name the crusades. *([4.3](lessons/04-03-the-crusades.md))*
- **Lateran IV did not invent confession or the "yellow badge"**, nor create the Inquisition (canon 3 set anti-heresy measures; papal inquisitors came in the 1230s). *([5.1](lessons/05-01-papal-monarchy-innocent-iii-to-boniface-viii.md))*
- **"The Inquisition" was not one body in this period**: say "papal inquisitors" or name the tribunal. *([5.3](lessons/05-03-heresy-inquisition-and-the-jews.md))*
- **Relinquishment is not innocence.** *([5.3](lessons/05-03-heresy-inquisition-and-the-jews.md))*
- **Don't fold the Jews into heresy**: the Talmud trial and expulsions came through papal letters and royal power. *([5.3](lessons/05-03-heresy-inquisition-and-the-jews.md))*
- **A doubtful quotation acquits no one** ("Kill them all"); a low execution rate is not a low punishment rate (Gui). *([5.3](lessons/05-03-heresy-inquisition-and-the-jews.md))*
- **Name each persecution's charge**: Mary's victims died under heresy law, most of Elizabeth's under treason statutes; count both. *([6.3](lessons/06-03-the-english-reformations.md))*
- **"The Inquisition" is three institutions plus the medieval one**; Rome is not evidence about Spain. **It did not try Jews for being Jews**; the expulsions were royal, but leaving the tribunal out of their story is also an error. *([6.5](lessons/06-05-the-spanish-and-roman-inquisitions.md))*
- **The witch-hunt was not mainly inquisitorial.** **The Kamen–Lea dispute is not about body counts.** **Don't read the Day of Pardon back as the historians' verdict.** *([6.5](lessons/06-05-the-spanish-and-roman-inquisitions.md))*
- **"Mostly disease" is not "no one's fault"**: Hispaniola's collapse began before smallpox. *([7.1](lessons/07-01-conquest-and-conscience-in-the-americas.md))*
- **"The concordat made Hitler legitimate" / "saved the German Church"** are causal claims the dates alone cannot carry. **Victim and ally at once** (Spain). **Don't flatten the archives** (2020 added documents on both sides). *([9.1](lessons/09-01-the-church-and-the-totalitarian-states.md))*
- **"Allegations" is not "findings"**, and the count is a floor. **The Day of Pardon was not a response to the abuse crisis.** **"Vatican II abolished Latin" is wrong.** *([9.3](lessons/09-03-after-the-council.md))*

### Science and the Galileo myth

- **"Galileo was condemned as a heretic"**: vehemently suspected, the middle grade. *([7.3](lessons/07-03-the-galileo-affair.md))*
- **"Galileo had proved the earth moves"**: not in 1633; Tycho fitted, the tides argument was wrong; aberration (1729) and parallax (1838) came later. *([7.3](lessons/07-03-the-galileo-affair.md))*
- **"The reversal came in 1992"**: the books came back 1758–1835; 1992 added an admission that the theologians erred, without the word "rehabilitation". *([7.3](lessons/07-03-the-galileo-affair.md))*

---

## Conventions

- **One card per course**, covering every lesson. The linter checks that every lesson file is cited somewhere on this card.
- **Intuition first**, same as lessons: a plain-English line before the facts.
- **Headings are anchors.** Renaming a `###` breaks inbound lesson links; the linter catches it, but prefer not to rename. Headings are plain ASCII (no dashes other than hyphens, no parentheses, no diacritics) so their slugs stay stable.
- **Hedges are load-bearing.** Where a lesson gives a range, "c.", "disputed" or "about", the card does too. Never harden a contested number or date to make a problem come out cleanly.
- **Verdict-neutral.** The cruxes are maps, not answers; the primacy table states what each piece of evidence does and does not show and leaves what it proves to the theology courses.
- **Boss problems stay unsolved here.** Where an entry says "Boss N" or "outline only", the card gives the terrain and stops.
- **Levels, not verdicts.** Where a doctrinal claim's weight matters, cite its level from [`fundamental-theology`](../fundamental-theology/lessons/04-04-the-ladder-of-doctrinal-authority.md); this course never assigns one.
- **No prose dollar signs** (see CLAUDE.md).
- Length is not capped the way a lesson's is: this is a lookup surface, not a read-through. But every line still has to earn its place.
